## Personality
*Source: `personality.html` (Part 1)*

Personality includes all social and interpersonal abilities. While some abilities in other categories involve interacting with people (such as Masquerade), Personality abilities provide ways to manipulate and inspire others specifically. Characters well-versed in them feel strong and capable whenever they have other people to work with and recruit to their cause.

**Bow Out (Level 1) [Social] [Encounter]**

You excuse yourself from the conversation, shifting some of the focus of the discussion onto yourself in a way that makes your friends look a bit better.

**Action**: Your turn in a social contest.

**Effects**: Make a Maneuvering social statement against the other party, and if you flip a Spade and succeed you gain an Extra Success. If you succeed, the next time your party would gain Demerits they ignore one of those Demerits, and you are removed from the social contest.

**Prereqs**: (Persuasion or Rapport) 2, Insight 1

**Brawn Over Banter (Level 1) [Social] [Encounter]**

"...because of which I would strongly urge that you consider our offer."

"Yes, yes, I've heard all of this before, and I still fail to see... the reason why I would..."

"Is there a problem?"

"I... no, but it's quite difficult to sort this business out while your shirtless friend over there keeps flexing his muscles..."

"Then we had best wrap this up quickly, no? Trust me, he can do that all day."

**Action**: Your turn in a social contest.

**Effects**: Make a Maneuvering social statement against the other party, using Might as the relevant Skill. If you flip a Spade and succeed, you gain an Extra Success.

**Prereqs**: Might 2, Insight 1

**Challenge (Level 1) [Martial] [Encounter]**

You bellow out a challenge to an enemy, drawing their attention away from your allies and forcing them to face you.

**Action**: 1 AP.

**Target**: A hostile creature within [Presence Skill Total] meters.

**Effects**: Make a Presence attack against the target's Mental Defense. If the attack hits, you Taunt the target.

**Prereqs**: Presence 2, Integrity 1

**Fast Talker (Level 1) [Social] [Encounter]**

Sometimes when you get talking you just keep going on and on... and nobody can seem to get a word in edgewise.

**Action**: During your turn in a social contest, once per turn.

**Cost**: 1 Focus.

**Effects**: You may immediately take an extra turn in the current social contest. For this extra turn you are considered to be the front member of your party, but are not considered to have any back members.

**Prereqs**: (Presence, Persuasion, or Rapport) 3, Integrity 2

**Menacing Insinuation (Level 1) [Social] [Encounter]**

"Entirely unrelated to the current matter, but I think the dust scorpion is a fascinating creature; did you know its venom can pass into the body by mere touch? A century ago it was a favored tool of merchant princes, whose spies would use the venom to kill the families of competitors who refused trade agreements with those princes. Its use is so hard to tell, we only know about the practice from memoirs - the assassins were never caught. But anyway, I apologize for rambling, back to our negotiations..."

**Action**: Your turn in a social contest.

**Effects**: Make a Forceful social statement against the other party, using Streetwise as the relevant Skill. If you flip a Spade and succeed, you gain an Extra Success.

**Prereqs**: Streetwise 2, Integrity 1

**"Well, Actually..." (Level 1) [Social] [Encounter]**

Having read entire shelves of books on some of the most obscure topics, you can call up factoids and figures from your studies so that when someone tries to argue with you, you can correct them on the spot.

**Action**: Your turn in a social contest.

**Effects**: Make a Debate social statement against the other party, using Academics as the relevant Skill. If you flip a Spade and succeed, you gain an Extra Success.

**Prereqs**: Academics 2, Insight 1

**Storied Adventurer (Level 1)**

Word of your deeds, adventures, and accomplishments have a tendency to make it to local taverns, retold by all kinds of folk. While not the actions of a great hero, nonetheless you make for a bit of an inspiring figure that people can't help but warm up to a bit.

**Passive**: This Technique applies to you at all times.

**Effects**: As long as you introduce yourself, you enjoy the following benefits:

Common folk are likely to give you the benefit of the doubt, easing their suspicions

When on an important quest, sometimes people will offer you simple supplies, or other aid

Your drinks are frequently on the house

**Prereqs**: Rapport 2, Performance 1

**Witty Quote (Level 1) [Social] [Encounter]**

Erudite and practiced in theatrics, you have a flair for quoting books and plays in the middle of discussions, catching those who disagree with you quite off-guard.

**Action**: Your turn in a social contest.

**Effects**: Make a Debate social statement against the other party, using Performance as the relevant Skill. If you flip a Spade and succeed, you gain an Extra Success.

**Prereqs**: Performance 2, Integrity 1

**Corner and Interrogate (Level 2) [Social] [Encounter]**

"No, excuse me, I was talking to your friend and I would appreciate it if you'd stop trying to interrupt. She's a big girl, you don't have to stand up for her, let her speak for herself."

**Action**: Your turn in a social contest, while you are the front member of your party.

**Effects**: Make a Debate social statement against the other party, and if you flip a Spade and succeed you gain an Extra Success. If you succeed, both parties must use the same front members on their next turns.

**Prereqs**: Persuasion 3, Insight 2

**Cry of Victory (Level 2) [Martial] [Encounter]**

As you strike down an enemy you bellow out, shaking your foes to their bones and causing them to briefly hesitate.

**Action**: 1 AP - Interrupt (you Down a hostile creature).

**Target**: Any number of hostile creatures within [Presence Skill Total] meters.

**Effects**: Make a Presence attack against the Mental Defense of each target. Each target hit is Frightened of you.

**Prereqs**: Presence 3, Might 1, Performance 1

**Gritty Countenance (Level 2) [Social] [Encounter]**

Whether you're at a podium having a debate or sitting at a tavern glaring at someone from behind your friend's back, your stern demeanor makes people think twice before criticizing you or your friends too harshly.

**Action**: Your turn in a social contest.

**Effects**: Make a Forceful social statement against the other party, using Presence as the relevant Skill. If you flip a Spade and succeed, you gain an Extra Success. If you succeed, until your party's next turn your front member may use

8 + [your Presence Skill Total]

in place of any of their Defenses.

**Prereqs**: Presence 3, Resilience 2

**Heartfelt Diplomacy (Level 2) [Social] [Encounter]**

You can easily give others the impression that you're easygoing, friendly, and relatable. It's no surprise that in the middle of prolonged discussions, they find themselves perfectly in sync with your emotional wavelength.

**Action**: Your turn in a social contest.

**Effects**: Make a Maneuvering social statement against the other party, using Rapport as the relevant Skill. If you flip a Spade and succeed you gain an Extra Success. If you succeed, the other party may only make social statements with Rapport for the rest of the social contest. This effect ends if any of your party members make a social statement with a Skill other than Rapport.

**Prereqs**: Rapport 3, Insight 2

**Instill Emotion (Level 2)**

Your songs, poems, and speeches are so powerful and moving that they are capable of inspiration bordering on mind control. Rare is the man who cannot be brought to tears by one of your melancholy dirges.

**Action**: 10 minutes.

**Effects**: Choose an emotion you would like to influence bystanders towards and make a Performance flip with a difficulty based on the crowd's current mood; if you flip a Spade and succeed then you gain an Extra Success. If you succeed then the overall mood of the social venue will shift based on the emotion you chose. This lasts for the scene, plus roughly 10 more minutes for each Extra Success. Depending on the situation, the emotion might be stronger and/or might last beyond the usual duration.

**Prereqs**: Performance 3

**Muscles Make Morale (Level 2) [Martial] [Encounter]**

Invoking tales of great warriors and flexing your muscles inspiringly, you wash away your comrades' worries and concern and push them to battle.

**Action**: 1 AP.

**Target**: Up to [half your Performance Skill Total] allies who can see or hear you.

**Effects**: For each target, choose one:

Remove a stack of Taunted or Frightened from the target

The target is Hasted once

**Prereqs**: Performance 3, Might 2

**Undermine Confidence (Level 2) [Social] [Encounter]**

"No, I don't quite agree with your stance. You've fundamentally misunderstood every factor at play here, and furthermore you greatly overestimate your leverage in this situation. I'll humor you by reiterating my offer once more, and if you want to keep stubbornly insisting you deserve more than I am willing to give in this exchange then it will make it crystal clear to me you are not worth dealing with."

**Action**: Your turn in a social contest.

**Effects**: Make a social statement against the other party, and if you flip a Spade and succeed you gain an Extra Success. If you succeed, note the type of social statement you made - the next time one of the other party's front members makes a social statement of that type this social contest, they have Bad Luck on it.

**Prereqs**: (Presence, Persuasion, or Rapport) 3, Insight 2

**Bolstering Refrain (Level 3) [Encounter]**

Your words and songs uplift your allies, pushing away their weariness and straightening their backs to fight anew.

**Action**: 1 AP.

**Cost**: 1 Focus.

**Target**: Up to [half your Performance Skill Total] allies who can see or hear you.

**Effects**: Each target heals 1 Health.

**Prereqs**: Performance 4

**Bully Into Submission (Level 3) [Social] [Encounter]**

By gauging someone's insecurities and whittling away at their confidence, over the course of a discussion you can make even the most headstrong people in the room end up sulking in the corner for the evening.

**Action**: Your turn in a social contest.

**Effects**: Make a Forceful social statement against the other party using Presence, and if you flip a Spade and succeed you gain an Extra Success. If you succeed, choose one member of the other party and note who they are. If you win the social contest then that person will remove themselves from the scene as much as they reasonably  can, and for at least the rest of the day they will act more subdued and withdrawn. Left to their own devices they will most likely recover, but other events might influence their mood more negatively and with more long-lasting consequences.

**Prereqs**: Presence 4, Insight 3

**Exert Pressure (Level 3) [Martial] [Encounter]**

Standing strong in combat, flexing, your presence in a pitched melee is impossible to ignore.

**Action**: 1 AP.

**Target**: Any number of creatures adjacent to you.

**Effects**: Make a Presence attack against the Mental Defense of each target. Each target hit is Taunted by you once.

**Prereqs**: Presence 4, Integrity 2, Performance 1

**Leading Question (Level 3) [Social] [Encounter]**

You've mastered the art of setting someone up in a conversation; anyone who discusses sensitive matters with you unwary of your skill may easily end up revealing something they'd rather stay secret.

**Action**: Your turn in a social contest.

**Effects**: Make a Maneuvering social statement against the other party, and if you flip a Spade and succeed you gain an Extra Success. If you succeed and ultimately win the social contest, that person lets slip a piece of information about the subject of the social contest about which they were otherwise trying to avoid mentioning or keep secret. This might be something they were lying about, trying to omit, or speaking in circles around, but it must be specifically related to the topic that was discussed. Based on the social contest outcome you choose, that person may be willing - or coerced into - giving more information about the subject.

**Prereqs**: (Persuasion or Rapport) 4, Insight 3

**New Best Friend (Level 3) [Social] [Encounter]**

"Oh yeah, guys, meet Ben! We've been talking over drinks all night, did you know he's the guard captain around here? Ben, show 'em your sword; guys isn't that the nicest blade you've seen since we were at the Duke's last month? Anyways, I told Ben here about how we're looking for the one guy, and he was thinking maybe we could stop by the barracks tomorrow and flip through some files he has that might have some stuff about the one incident with the goats that started all of this."

**Action**: Your turn in a social contest.

**Effects**: Make a Maneuvering or Forceful social statement against the other party using Rapport, and if you flip a Spade and succeed you gain an Extra Success. If you succeed, ultimately win the social contest, and choose Friendly as the outcome, even after the scene ends the shift in the target's opinion from the outcome will be permanent and from that point on. Unless you do something to change their opinion then they will treat you with a favorable attitude, as though you are a friend whose approval they value.

**Prereqs**: Rapport 4, Insight 3

**Tactician's Waltz (Level 3) [Martial] [Encounter]**

Skirmishes are a lot like formal dances; when following the lead of a skilled dancer, the most brilliant of spins and turns looks effortless.

**Action**: 1 AP.

**Target**: Any number of willing allies who can see or hear you.

**Effects**: You may adjust the targets' positions in turn order as you choose, but by no more than a total of [half your Performance Skill Total] positions.

**Prereqs**: Performance 4, Academics 2, Insight 1

**Raise Spirits (Level 4)**

Even though you may not know how to do everything, you know how to inspire your friends to do anything.

**Passive**: This Technique applies to you at all times.

**Effects**: You may use Performance to give Backup to an ally, instead of the Skill used by that ally. When doing so, you have Good Luck on the flip to give Backup. This may let you give Backup in situations where you would not be able to do so with the Skill used by your ally.

For 2 AP - Interrupt (an ally within [Performance Skill Total] declares an attack), you may give them Good Luck on the attack.

**Prereqs**: Performance 3, Integrity 3, Presence 3

**Rally to Victory (Level 4)**

In every great war story, when the battle is grim and the army is on the brink of defeat a hero will rise above the masses and inspire them to victory. When your friends are struggling against a great foe you appear as something out of a storybook, the great hero who pushes his companions to greatness.

**Action**: 1 AP.

**Cost**: 1 Focus.

**Target**: Any number of willing allies who can see or hear you.

**Effects**: The targets draw any number of cards, up to a total of [half your Performance Skill Total] . You choose who draws how many cards. Then each of those targets discards a card for each one they drew.

**Prereqs**: Performance 5

**War Cry (Level 4) [Martial] [Encounter]**

A time-honored tradition across the land, from nomad barbarians to gladiators fighting in pits for the amusement of crowds, and even in negotiations in less respectable establishments, letting loose a ferocious scream can shake even the strongest fighters at times. Unfortunately, as Thorrgar the mighty berserker-turned-merchant learned, screaming about bathing in the entrails of someone rarely works out well on the senate floor.

**Action**: 1 AP.

**Target**: A creature within [Presence Skill Total] meters.

**Effects**: Make a Presence attack against the target's Mental Defense. If it hits, the target is Crippled 4 times.

**Prereqs**: Presence 5, Might 2, Performance 2

**The Gatekeeper (Level 5) [Martial] [Encounter]**

"Your entire division was defeated by that band of rebels?"

"It was their commander. She stood at the gate to their camp, attempting to block our way; as though we couldn't have easily scaled their walls! She spoke, in a calm voice that seemed to carry across the valley: "Through me, first". Our ladderbearers faltered, our archers couldn't take their eyes off of her. As our soldiers hesitated, a rebel battlemage from behind the walls sent fire raining onto our lines. They all broke and ran, constantly looking over their shoulders at the commander until they were back in the woods."

**Action**: 1 AP.

**Cost**: 1 Focus.

**Target**: Any number of creatures who can see or hear you.

**Effects**: Make a Presence attack against the Mental Defense of each target. Each target hit is Taunted by you 4 times.

**Prereqs**: Presence 5, Integrity 5, Performance 5

**Verbal Beatdown (Level 5) [Social] [Encounter]**

Using a variety of conversational techniques you meticulously deconstruct someone's argument as well as their choice of vocation and life choices, closing out with a scathing dismissal of everything they stand for.

**Action**: Your turn in a social contest.

**Cost**: 1 Focus.

**Effects**: Make a Maneuvering, Debate, and a Forceful social statement against the other party. For each of these, if you flip a Spade and succeed you gain an Extra Success.

**Prereqs**: Presence 5, Persuasion 5, Rapport 5

Cloak of Faces

Usually made of rough leather, this cloak is heavy, large, and features a spacious hood. It can be placed over the face of a recently-deceased humanoid, and after a full round it will permanently remove the corpse's face, leaving only bone and musculature underneath. After wearing the cloak for a full night - from dusk to dawn - the wearer becomes attuned to the cloak, lasting until another wearer becomes attuned to the cloak and granting access to the cloak's full power. While attuned, the wearer is aware of every face the cloak has ever stolen from a corpse, and the wearer can raise the hood as an Action and replace their face with any face that has been stolen by the cloak. This effect is not a mere illusion, causing the wearer's face to actually change features to that of the new face. This effect lasts until the hood is lowered, reverting the wearer's face to their usual face.

## Social Combat
*Source: `social-combat.html` (Part 1)*

Social Combat

First something happens - the party begins negotiations, or tries to talk their way through something, or whatever, just something more than a simple Skill flip. Inherently there has to be some kind of conflict or animosity between the parties, even something as simple as one party asking for information from someone who just wants to be left alone with their beer.

Social combat engages, people duke it out with words.

If the enemy wins, things generally get stonewalled. That NPC will probably not hear the party out, or some similar setback.

If the party wins, they've outmaneuvered the NPC in some manner. They will be forced to admit the party is right, or can't really come up with a good way to argue against the party, or are afraid or amicable towards them and no longer wish to stand in their way.

Regardless of the outcome, a person can only be in a party for social combat once per scene. (no retrysies)

Parties are declared, Agreement goals stated

Reflex flip for first social combat turn

Parties take turns making social attacks against each other; on a hit the other party suffers 1 social damage

If a party has more than one member, before their turn is declared they choose who will be their front member; everyone else is a back member

Once a party has had all of their members beaten, they lose (or the other wins, or whatever)

The winning party chooses their goal: Agreement goal, Embarassment, or Cowering/Friendly

When a social combat starts, you have the two parties. Each party is either one person or multiple people. If a party doesn't want to engage in social combat they can just decline at the very start, but that's basically just telling someone to go away if they try to chat you up, or as soon as you broach a certain topic they just shut down and tell you they're not discussing it.

Each party may then declare what their goal is in the combat - what information they're trying to glean, or what they're trying to convince someone to do. Usually just the instigator does this, but maybe sometimes both people do it. No mechanical impact, just helps define the scene.

Reflex flip to determine who goes first, then it's just a back and forth with no real "turn order".

There are a bunch of attacks people can make, using Persuasion, Diplomacy, Intimidate.

Specifically there are three types of social attacks, each of which with a corresponding social defense against which they are made:

If you hit with a social attack you inflict a social hit against the other party. If your party has more social hits than you have Integrity Skill Total / 2 then you may not take any actions during the social combat. If everyone in your party has more social hits than their social HP total your party loses the social combat.

Each turn your party chooses a front member, and all other members are back members.

Each back member can set up the front member, making a social attack against the other party. Each back member whose social attack hits gives their front member Good Luck on any social attacks they make this turn.

The front member makes a social attack on your turn. If the social attack hits the other party takes a social hit.

Until your next turn the other party's social attacks are against your front member's relevant defenses.

When a party loses social combat, they suffer a debuff or something decided by the winner:

**Agreement**: talk way past a bouncer, convince someone to do a fair business deal with you, whatever; could be as simple as dismissing the other party and refusing to talk any more on the subject

**Embarassment**: Your party has made the other party look foolish, unrefined, or otherwise at odds with the overall social fabric of your surroundings. All members of the other party have Bad Luck on any Persuasion, Diplomacy, or Intimidate flips they make this scene.

**Cowed**: The other party is cautious or afraid of your party. For the rest of the scene they will avoid you and are Frightened of your party.

**Friendly**: You have won the respect of the other party. As long as you do nothing to upset them they will give you their undivided attention and might go out of their way to help you for the scene, although they will not take more than a small risk on your behalf. They might risk upsetting someone else, but they won't (usually) do anything illegal if there's a chance they will be caught.

A party may choose to end social combat pre-emptively?

Is it different if someone just tries to stab someone else?

## Social Contest Techniques
*Source: `social-contest-techniques.html` (Part 1)*

Social Contest Techniques

**Blunt Comment (Level 1) [Social] [Encounter]**

You make a pointed, straightforward comment to someone; if you do it well you can catch them off-guard with your directness, but a clever opponent can call you out on it, embarrassing you slightly.

**Action**: Your turn in a social contest.

**Effects**: Make a Maneuvering social attack against the other party. If the attack hits, the other party suffers a Demerit. If the attack misses, your party suffers a Demerit.

If you flip a Spade for the attack and it hits, you may draw a card. If you do, at the end of the social contest you discard a card.

**Prereqs**: (Persuasion or Diplomacy) 3, Insight 2

**Bow Out (Level 1) [Social] [Encounter]**

You excuse yourself from the conversation, shifting some of the focus of the discussion onto yourself in a way that makes your friends look a bit better.

**Action**: Your turn in a social contest.

**Effects**: Make a Maneuvering social attack against the other party. If the attack hits, remove one of your party's Demerits and then you are removed from the social contest.

**Prereqs**: (Persuasion or Diplomacy) 3, Insight 2

**Brawn Over Banter (Level 1)**

[Social] [Encounter]

"...because of which I would strongly urge that you consider our offer."

"Yes, yes, I've heard all of this before, and I still fail to see... the reason why I would..."

"Is there a problem?"

"I... no, but it's quite difficult to sort this business out while your shirtless friend over there keeps flexing his muscles..."

"Then we had best wrap this up quickly, no? Trust me, he can do that all day."

**Action**: Your turn in a social contest.

**Effects**: Make a Forceful social attack against the other party; this attack uses Might as its relevant Skill.

If you flip a Spade for the attack and it hits, you may draw a card. If you do, at the end of the social contest you discard a card.

**Prereqs**: Might 3, Insight 2

**Gritty Countenance (Level 1) [Social] [Encounter]**

Whether you're at a podium having a debate or sitting at a tavern glaring at someone from behind your friend's back, your stern demeanor makes people think twice before criticizing you or your friends

too

harshly.

**Action**: Your turn in a social contest.

**Effects**: Make a Forceful social attack against the other party; this attack uses Intimidate as its relevant Skill. If the attack hits, until your party's next turn your front member may use

8 + [your Intimidate Skill Total]

in place of any of their Defenses.

If you flip a Spade for the attack and it hits, you may draw a card. If you do, at the end of the social contest you discard a card.

**Prereqs**: Intimidate 3, Stamina 2

**Menacing Insinuation (Level 1) [Social] [Encounter]**

"Entirely unrelated to the current matter, but I think the dust scorpion is a fascinating creature; did you know its venom can pass into the body by mere touch? A century ago it was a favored tool of merchant princes, whose spies would use the venom to kill the families of competitors who refused trade agreements with those princes. Its use is so hard to tell, we only know about the practice from memoirs - the assassins were never caught. But anyway, I apologize for rambling, back to our negotiations..."

**Action**: Your turn in a social contest.

**Effects**: Make a Maneuvering social attack against the other party; this attack uses Savvy as its relevant Skill.

If you flip a Spade for the attack and it hits, you may draw a card. If you do, at the end of the social contest you discard a card.

**Prereqs**: Savvy 3, Insight 2

**"Well, Actually..." (Level 1) [Social] [Encounter]**

Having read entire shelves of books on some of the most obscure topics, you can call up factoids and figures from your studies so that when someone tries to argue with you, you can correct them on the spot.

**Action**: Your turn in a social contest.

**Effects**: Make a Debate social attack against the other party; this attack uses Academics as its relevant Skill.

If you flip a Spade for the attack and it hits, you may draw a card. If you do, at the end of the social contest you discard a card.

**Prereqs**: Academics 3, Insight 2

**Witty Quote (Level 1) [Social] [Encounter]**

Erudite and practiced in theatrics, you have a flair for quoting books and plays in the middle of discussions, catching those who disagree with you quite off-guard.

**Action**: Your turn in a social contest.

**Effects**: Make a Maneuvering social attack against the other party; this attack uses Perform as its relevant Skill.

If you flip a Spade for the attack and it hits, you may draw a card. If you do, at the end of the social contest you discard a card.

**Prereqs**: Perform 3, Academics 2

**Brutal Embarassment (Level 2) [Social] [Encounter]**

By gauging someone's insecurities and whittling away at their confidence, over the course of a discussion you can make even the most headstrong people in the room end up sulking in the corner for the evening.

**Action**: Your turn in a social contest.

**Effects**: Make a Debate social attack against the other party, and if the attack hits then note who the front member of the other party is. If you win the social contest then that person will remove themselves from the scene as much as is reasonable and for at least the rest of the day they will fall into a deep melancholy, although it may last longer than that. Left to their own they will most likely recover, but other events might influence their mood accordingly.

If you flip a Spade for the attack and it hits, you may draw a card. If you do, at the end of the social contest you discard a card.

**Prereqs**: (Persuasion or Diplomacy) 3, Insight 2

Corner and Interrogate (Level 2) [Social] [Encounter] "No, excuse me, I was talking to your friend and I would appreciate it if you'd stop trying to interrupt. He's a big boy, you don't have to stand up for him, let him speak for himself."

**Action**: Your turn in a social contest.

**Effects**: Make a Debate social attack against the other party. If the attack hits then note who the front member of the other party is; on the other party's next turn if that person is not the other party's front member, the other party suffers a Demerit.

If you flip a Spade for the attack and it hits, you may draw a card. If you do, at the end of the social contest you discard a card.

**Prereqs**: Persuasion 3, Insight 2

Instill Emotion (Level 2) [Social] [Encounter] Your music, speeches, or other performances are so powerful and moving that they are capable of inspiration that borders on mind control. Rare is the man who cannot be brought to tears by one of your melancholy dirges.

**Action**: This Technique is used as part of an action used to Perform, usually over a few minutes or so.

**Target**: All creatures who can hear or see your performance, as appropriate.

**Effects**: Choose an emotion you would like to influence bystanders towards, then make a Forceful social attack against the other party; this attack uses Perform as its relevant Skill. If the attack hits and your party eventually wins the social contest, the overall mood of the social venue will shift based on the emotion you chose. If you instill sadness in a happy tavern crowd the mood will change to a neutral or even sullen one, if you instill anger in a gathered crowd they may be incited to riot, and if you incite courage in a group of soldiers they will be more likely to stand their ground against a superior force.

If you flip a Spade for the attack and it hits, you may draw a card. If you do, at the end of the social contest you discard a card.

**Prereqs**: Perform 3, Insight 2

Leading Question (Level 2) [Social] [Encounter] You've mastered the art of setting someone up in a conversation; anyone who discusses sensitive matters with you unwary of your skill may easily end up revealing something they'd rather stay secret.

**Action**: Your turn in a social contest.

**Effects**: Make a Maneuvering social attack against the other party, and if the attack hits then note who the front member of the other party is. If you win the social contest then that person lets slip a piece of information about the subject of the social contest about which they were otherwise trying to avoid mentioning or keep secret. This might be something they were lying about, trying to omit, or speaking in circles around, but it must be specifically related to the topic that was discussed.

If you flip a Spade for the attack and it hits, you may draw a card. If you do, at the end of the social contest you discard a card.

**Prereqs**: (Persuasion or Diplomacy) 3, Insight 2

**Logos Overwhelming (Level 2) [Social] [Encounter]**

With your study in formal logic and philosophical reasoning, you can thoroughly shut down an opponent's argument.

**Action**: Your turn in a social contest.

**Effects**: Make a Debate social attack against the other party. If the attack hits then the other party has Disadvantage on the next Debate social attack that any of their front members make this social contest.

If you flip a Spade for the attack and it hits, you may draw a card. If you do, at the end of the social contest you discard a card.

**Prereqs**: Persuasion 3, Academics 2

**New Best Friend (Level 2) [Social] [Encounter]**

"Oh yeah, guys, meet Ben! We've been talking over drinks all night, did you know he's the guard captain around here? Ben, show 'em your sword; guys isn't that the nicest blade you've seen since we were at the Duke's last month? Anyway, I told Ben here about how we're looking for the one guy, and he was thinking maybe we could stop by the barracks tomorrow and flip through some files he has that might have some stuff about the one incident with the goats that started all of this."

**Action**: Your turn in a social contest.

**Effects**: Make a Maneuvering social attack against the other party. If the attack hits then if you win the social contest and choose Friendly as your goal, even after the scene ends this shift in the target's opinion will be permanent and from that point on, unless you do something to change their opinion, they will treat you with a favorable attitude as though you are a friend whose approval they value.

If you flip a Spade for the attack and it hits, you may draw a card. If you do, at the end of the social contest you discard a card.

**Prereqs**: Diplomacy 3, Persuasion 2

**Rumormonger (Level 3) [Social] [Encounter]**

You know all the ways a rumor can spread, and are adept at starting rumors then fanning them into wildfires.

**Cost**: 1 Soul.

**Action**: 1 minute.

**Target**: The general social crowd present while in a larger social situation such as a formal party, moderately crowded tavern, or town hall.

**Effects**: You attempt to start a rumor, making a Persuasion or Savvy flip to determine how widely spread and believed this rumor is over the course of the following few days or weeks. Based on the circumstances surrounding the subject of the rumor, and how believable it is, the effectiveness of the rumor and the difficulty for the flip will vary.

Once you have successfully spread a rumor, you may take advantage of it during your turn in a social contest. This gives you Good Luck on one social attack, and if you flip a Spade for that attack and it hits you may draw a card; if you draw a card this way, at the end of the social contest you discard a card. The rumor must somehow be relevant to the matter at hand in order to take advantage of it, and you may only do so once per social contest.

**Prereqs**: Persuasion 3, Savvy 3

**Undermine Confidence (Level 3) [Social] [Encounter]**

"No, I don't quite agree with your stance. You've fundamentally misunderstood every factor at play here, and furthermore you greatly overestimate your leverage in this situation. I'll humor you by reiterating my offer once more, and if you want to keep stubbornly insisting you deserve more than I am willing to give in this exchange then it will make it crystal clear to me you are not worth dealing with."

**Action**: Your turn in a social contest.

**Effects**: Make a social attack against the other party, and note the type of social attack you made. If the attack hits then the other party has Disadvantage on all social attacks of that type for the rest of the social contest.

If you flip a Spade for the attack and it hits, you may draw a card. If you do, at the end of the social contest you discard a card.

**Prereqs**: Insight 3, Persuasion 3

**Verbal Beatdown (Level 5) [Social] [Encounter]**

Using a variety of verbal techniques you meticulously deconstruct someone's argument as well as their choice of vocation and life choices, closing out with a blistering condemnation of everything they're arguing for.

**Cost**: 1 Soul.

**Action**: Your turn in a social contest.

**Effects**: Make a Maneuvering, Debate, and a Forceful social attack against the other party.

For each of these attacks, if you flip a Spade for the attack and it hits you may draw a card, and if you do at the end of the social contest you discard a card.

**Prereqs**:

Insight 5,

Persuasion 5, (Diplomacy or Intimidate) 5

## Social Contests
*Source: `social-contests.html` (Part 2)*

Sometimes social interactions go beyond a simple conversation. When matters go beyond a simple interaction and become a drawn-out discussion or argument, social contest rules can come into play.

First an event kicks the social contest off - the party begins negotiation, or tries to talk their way past a bouncer, or something. The key factor is that the party is now in conflict with another person or group, and wants to get their way. If the party wins then they've outmaneuvered the other group in some manner - that group admits the party is right, no longer has a way to argue against the party, is afraid of being in the party's way, or has taken a liking to the party and will help them.

Regardless of the outcome of a social contest, a person can only take part in one per scene.

Social Statements, Defenses, and Demerits

In a social contest, each party takes turns making social statements which are opposed by the other party's social defenses. A social statement is a Skill flip with a difficulty equal to the other party's social defense. The Skills that you can use for the flip and the social defense the flip is against are both determined by the type of social statement being made.

If you succeed on a social statement and you are a back member of your party, your front member gains Good Luck on all social statements they make this turn, and they gain Good Luck another time for each Extra Success you get. If you succeed on a social statement and you are the front member of your party, the other party gains a Demerit, plus an extra Demerit for each Extra Success you get.

If your party has more Demerits than your Integrity Skill Total, you can no longer act in the social contest. If your entire party can no longer act in the social contest, your party loses the social contest.

Maneuvering Statement

Leading the other party on, you try to frame the situation in a way that makes them more inclined to agree with you.

**Relevant Skill**: Persuasion or Rapport

**Targeted Defense**: Suspicion

Debate Statement

Posing a question or conclusion in a strictly argumentative way, you try to throw the other party off by making them justify themselves.

**Relevant Skill**: Persuasion

**Targeted Defense**: Argument

Forceful Statement

Using a raw appeal to emotion, you try to win over the hearts of the other party... or just intimidate them into giving in to you.

**Relevant Skill**: Rapport or Presence

**Targeted Defense**: Mental

The Course of a Social Contest

When a social contest begins, first the two different parties are determined. A party can be made of one or more people, and usually the two parties in a social contest are based on whatever interaction spawned the social contest. Then each party may state a goal for the social contest - it isn't necessary (or possible, in some cases) but it helps to explain what each party is trying to accomplish with the social contest.

Everyone in the social contest then makes a Reflex flip, each party chooses one of their members, and that member's Reflex result is used for the entire party. The party with the higher result takes the first turn in the social contest and must use their chosen member as their front member for that turn, and the party with the lower result uses their chosen member as their front member until the start of their turn.

On a party's turn they choose one member - until the start of their next turn that is their front member and all others in the party are back members. Each back member then gets a turn in any order, followed by the party's front member. A member may use their turn to make a social statement against the other party or use a Technique.

There are three ways that social contest can end:

Mutual Ending

When both parties agree to end the social contest, or when some outside event interrupts the social contest, then the social contest ends and neither party suffers any consequences as a result.

Forcible Ending

When one party deliberately interrupts and ends the social contest, violently or otherwise, that party suffers Embarrassment

(see below)

. That party may also suffer additional consequences depending on how they interrupt the social contest.

One Party Wins

When a party wins the social contest they may choose what outcome will occur: Agreement, Embarrassment, Cowed, or Friendly.

Results of a Social Contest

Agreement

The winning party has convinced the other party to agree with them or otherwise accomplish the goal they declared at the start of social contest. The exact nature of this outcome will depend on the goal declared and the overall situation, but will represent that the other party has genuinely been convinced to agree with the winning party, or at least work with them towards their goal.

Embarassment

The winning party has made the other party look foolish, unrefined, or otherwise at odds with the overall social fabric of the surroundings. All members of the other party have Bad Luck on any Persuasion, Diplomacy, or Intimidate flips they make this scene, and may suffer additional consequences.

Cowed

The other party is cautious or afraid of the winning party. For the rest of the scene they will avoid all members of the winning party where possible and are Frightened of them.

Friendly

The winning party has won the respect of the other party. As long as nothing happens to upset them then for the scene the members of the other party they will give the members of the winning party their undivided attention and might go out of their way to help them, although they will not take more than a small risk on behalf of the winning party. They might risk upsetting someone else, but they won't (usually) do anything illegal if there's a chance they will be caught.

## Social Contests
*Source: `social-contests_1.html` (Part 2)*

Sometimes social interactions go beyond a simple conversation. When matters go beyond a normal interaction and become a full-blown discussion or argument, the outcome of the scene is determined using social contest rules.

First something happens - the party begins negotiations, or tries to talk their way through something, or whatever, just something more than a simple Skill flip. Inherently there has to be some kind of conflict or animosity between the parties, even something as simple as one party asking for information from someone who just wants to be left alone with their beer.

If the party wins, they've outmaneuvered the other group in some manner. They will be forced to admit the party is right, or can't really come up with a good way to argue against the party, or are either afraid of or amicable towards the party and no longer wish to stand in their way.

Regardless of the outcome, a person can only take part in social contest once per scene.

Social Contest Statistics

There are three basic social attacks that people can make in social contest, each of which is made against a specific social defense. Each social attack is made with a specific Skill from the relevant Skills for that attack, and each social defense is based on a different Skill:

Each member in social contest also has a Demerit Threshold of

[Integrity Skill Total] / 2.

Beginning Social Contests

When a social contest begins, first the two different parties are determined. A party can be made of one or more people, and usually the two parties in a social contest are based on whatever interaction spawned the social contest.

Once the two parties and all of their members are determined, each party may state a goal for the social contest. This is not necessary, but helps to both frame the narrative of the social contest and can influence the outcome of the social contest if that party wins, and so oftentimes the party that initiated the social contest will declare a goal that involves the other party in some way. Whether or not a goal is possible will depend on the situation at hand.

All members of both parties then make Reflex flips, and each party chooses one of their members whose results to use. The party with the higher result takes the first turn in the social contest and must use their chosen member as their front member for that turn. The party with the lower result uses their chosen member as their front member until the start of their turn.

A Party's Turn

On a party's turn they choose one of their members to be their front member until the start of their next turn, and all other members of the party are back members for the turn. Each back member then gets a turn in whichever order they choose, followed by the party's front member.

Any party member may use their turn to make a social attack against the other party. When a social attack is made against a party the difficulty of the attack is equal to the relevant social defense of that party's front member, and if the flip succeeds then the attack hits.

If a party member hits with a social attack it has different effects depending on whether or not that person is a front member or back member. If a back member hits with a social attack, their party's front member gains a +1 bonus on any social attacks they make this turn. If a front member hits with a social attack, in addition to any other effects the attack might have the other party gains a Demerit.

Demerits and Ending Social Contests

If your party has more Demerits than your Demerit Threshold, you do not get a turn on your party's turn. If, due to this, nobody in your party gets a turn then your party loses the social contest and the other party wins.

There are three ways that social contest can end:

**Mutual Ending**: When either both parties agree to end the social contest or when some outside event interrupts the social contest then the social contest ends and neither party suffers any consequences as a result.

**Forcible Ending**: When one party deliberately interrupts and ends the social contest, violently or otherwise, that party suffers Embarrassment

(see below)

. In addition, that party may suffer additional consequences depending on how they interrupt the social contest.

**One Party Wins**: When a party wins the social contest they may choose what outcome will occur.

The possible outcomes from social contest are Agreement, Embarrassment, Cowed, and Friendly.

**Agreement**: The winning party has convinced the other party to agree with them or otherwise accomplish the goal they declared at the start of social contest. The exact nature of this outcome will depend on the goal declared and the overall situation, but will represent that the other party has genuinely been convinced to agree with the winning party, or at least work with them towards their goal.

**Embarrassment**: The winning party has made the other party look foolish, unrefined, or otherwise at odds with the overall social fabric of the surroundings. All members of the other party have Bad Luck on any Persuasion, Diplomacy, or Intimidate flips they make this scene, and may suffer additional consequences.

**Cowed**: The other party is cautious or afraid of the winning party. For the rest of the scene they will avoid all members of the winning party where possible and are Frightened of them.

**Friendly**: The winning party has won the respect of the other party. A

s long as nothing happens to upset them then for the scene the members of the other party they will give the members of the winning party their undivided attention and

might go out of their way to help them, although they will not take more than a small risk on behalf of the winning party.

They might risk upsetting someone else, but they won't (usually) do anything illegal if there's a chance they will be caught.

## Social Encounters
*Source: `social-encounters.html` (Part 1)*

Social encounters represent a situation where the party needs to convince or win someone over - to be allowed entrance somewhere, to gain information, or earn an ally to their cause. An encounter is designed as a single NPC, and although they may have other NPCs participating and supporting them in the discussion these elements are represented as aspects of the primary NPC. A  'Social Proficiency', or SP, rating is used to show how overall competent an NPC is in a social situation and how well they can stonewall the party or chase them off; this rating goes from 0 to 10. This only represents their social ability, and is separate from any other measure of the NPC's power.

SP 0-3:

An NPC with this low of an SP could almost never convince the party of anything, would be trivially convinced or intimidated, and would likely never be put in charge of anything.

SP 4:

An average NPC, at least for those that would be in a situation to have anything the party might need. Moderately stubborn, clever, or otherwise requiring some finesse to win over. Likely on par with a starting character, and equally likely to be rolled over by a full party.

**SP 5-6**: R

**SP 8**: A

**SP 10**: A

NPC Social Opponent Design

NPCs designed for combat have simplified statistics that cover only what is relevant for a fight. Before designing an NPC you should figure out what general archetype they will follow - this could be as simple as 'balanced fighter' or as complicated as 'support magic user, physically tough, uses shadow'. It should at the very least include what their main strength will be in combat, as well as their overall theme. Once you have this determined, the steps to design an NPC combatant are:

Note the NPC's Combat Proficiency, or CP

Decide which Stats the NPC is focused on

Choose which attack(s) the NPC can use

Allocate the NPC's talents

Calculate the NPC's Health, Resists, Defenses, and Speed

Unless otherwise stated, an NPC has a Skill Total for any given Skill equal to half their CP, rounded down.

Health is written as one number, and once an NPC has lost half of their Health they are Wounded twice. By default, an enemy dies once they lose more Health than they have.

In theory it should be fairly simple to change NPC stats beyond this method. Especially for things like monsters, changing their statistics to end up with higher Resists but lower Defenses, for example, or designing NPCs with much lower Health but that are placed into encounters in greater numbers. This is simply a quick way to establish a baseline.

Initial Statistics

An NPC's Demerit Threshold is a stand-in for a PC's Integrity Skill Total, and starts at

[SP] - 2

.

An NPC's Defenses all start at

[SP] + 6.

An NPC's Statements all start at +

[SP] - 2

.

Demeanor

Determine the NPC's demeanor by ranking the following aspects in order:

**Calm**: Improves Demerit Threshold

**Clever**: Improves Debate Statement and Argument Defense

**Imposing**: - Improves Forceful Statement and Mental Defense

**Subtle**: Improves Maneuvering Statement and Suspicion Defense

The first aspect gives a +3 bonus to its statistics, the second gives a +2 bonus, the third a +1 bonus, and the fourth gives no bonus.

Edge

Finally, decide what edge the NPC has in this social contest. This is a bonus to the NPC that represents a favorable aspect of them, or of their surroundings.

Home Turf -

The venue where the contest is taking place is one the NPC is familiar with or frequents, and they can make outsiders feel rather unwelcome.

If the party gains any Demerits, their front member has Bad Luck on their statements on their next turn.

Ally -

The NPC has a loyal friend who stands by them.

The NPC's party has one other NPC in it, with an SP 1 lower than the main NPC's.

Lackeys -

The NPC has lackeys or supporters who back them up.

The NPC's party has two other NPCs in it, each with an SP 2 lower than the main NPC's.

All-Rounder -

The NPC is a well-rounded conversationalist with no obvious weaknesses in their statements.

The NPC's lowest Defense and Statement are each increased by 2

(factored into statistics)

.

High Stakes -

The social contest takes place in a shadow realm or some other supernatural context, where the conversation represents a battle of spirits instead of mere words.

If the party gains any Demerits, their front member loses that much Health.
